    📖 StackCalc Master Answer Key & Keystroke Verification Portal

    The Paper-First Principle: Calculators should never replace reasoning. Students first build the problem with physical 3D blocks or write their mathematical setup, solve on paper by hand, and verify on their distraction-free StackCalc instrument. This portal provides complete worked solutions and canonical keystrokes across all volumes in The StackCalc Library.

    📚 Quick Navigation by Book

    • Book 2A: Early Elementary Math Lab (Grades K–2 • SC-6 • Units 01–10)
    • Book 2B: Upper Elementary Math Lab (Grades 3–5 • SC-6 • Units 01–10)
    • Book 3: Middle School Math Lab (Grades 6–8 • SC-6 • Units 01–10)
    • Book 4: High School & STEM Lab (Grades 9–12 & AP • SC-32 • Units 01–10)
    • Book 5: Stack Speed & Fluency (Full-Year 180-Test Edition)

    📗 Book 2A: Early Elementary Math Lab (Grades K–2 • SC-6)

    Covers Units 01 through 10 plus 4 Cumulative Benchmark & Capstone Mastery Exams: Step Counting, Number Bonds, Make-10 Trades, Doubling, Place Value, Subtraction, Regrouping Carry, Two-Digit Operations, Equal Groups & Arrays, and Measurement/Money (840 active problems).

    Unit 01: Counting, Cardinality & The One-More Step Counter

    Grades: Pre-K – Kindergarten | Instrument: StackCalc SC-6 | Visual / Block Model: Place Value Studio (Digit Blocks 0–9 & Gold [+1] Step Tokens) Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.K.CC.A.1, CCSS.MATH.K.CC.B.4, CCSS.MATH.K.OA.A.1

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): Place the [4] Digit Block in the Ones column of your Place Value Tray and add 1 Gold [+1] step token. What total quantity do you have now?
    • Verified Answer: 5 ([5] Digit Block; 4 + 1 = 5).
    • Keystrokes: 4 ENTER 1 +
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Row 1 holds [6] and Row 0 holds [2] on your Place Value Tray. Which row has more, and how many more does it have?
    • Verified Answer: Row 1 has 4 more (6 - 2 = 4).
    • Keystrokes: 6 ENTER 2 -

    2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

    # Investigation Prompt Expected Solution Calculator Verification Keys
    1 Place [3] in the top row and [2] in the bottom row of your Place Value Tray. Combine both groups—what is the total? 5 3 ENTER 2 +
    2 You have 5 ones on the tray and take away 2. What Digit Block shows how many ones remain? 3 5 ENTER 2 -
    3 You have 3 blocks on the tray and take away all 3. How many blocks are left, and which Digit Block shows an empty tray? 0 (Zero) 3 ENTER 3 -
    4 Start with [1] in the Ones socket and add one more [+1] token three times in a row. What count do you reach? 4 1 ENTER 1 + 1 + 1 +

    4. 100-Point Unit Mastery Check

    # Category Assessment Prompt Points Expected Solution Verification Keys
    1 Core Fluency Step up 1 more on the Ten-Frame: 6 + 1 = 10 pts 7 6 ENTER 1 +
    2 Core Fluency Step up 2 times on the number track: 8 + 1 + 1 = 10 pts 10 8 ENTER 1 + 1 +
    3 Core Fluency Remove 1 block from the tray: 9 - 1 = 10 pts 8 9 ENTER 1 -
    4 Core Fluency Step back 2 times on the tray: 7 - 1 - 1 = 10 pts 5 7 ENTER 1 - 1 -
    5 Core Fluency Find the missing [+1] steps: 5 + [ ? ] = 8 10 pts 3 8 ENTER 5 -
    6 Core Fluency Follow the 2-step tray path: 4 + 3 - 1 = 10 pts 6 4 ENTER 3 + 1 -
    7 Application Emma has 6 crayons and gets 2 more. How many crayons in all? 10 pts 8 crayons 6 ENTER 2 +
    8 Application A shelf holds 9 books. 2 books are checked out. How many books remain? 10 pts 7 books 9 ENTER 2 -
    9 Application An ant walks 5 steps forward, 2 more forward, and 1 step back. Total steps from start? 10 pts 6 steps 5 ENTER 2 + 1 -
    10 Error Analysis Error Analysis: Ben says 1 more than 8 is 7 because he counted backward. Give the right answer. 10 pts 9 1 ENTER 8 +

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Garden Stepping Stone Trail

    Scenario: A landscape gardener designs a walking path with stone markers spaced 1 meter apart. Students calculate steps, distances, and total stones needed.

    • Case Question 1: Start at Stone 4 and lay 3 more stones. What stone number are you on?
    • Expected Result: 7 | Keystrokes: 4 ENTER 1 + 1 + 1 +
    • Case Question 2: A trail has 8 stones. A bird lands on stone 5. How many stones remain to the end?
    • Expected Result: 3 | Keystrokes: 8 ENTER 5 -
    • Case Question 3: Two parallel paths have 4 stones and 5 stones. How many stones total?
    • Expected Result: 9 | Keystrokes: 4 ENTER 5 +
    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: Leo says stepping forward 2 stones from 6 gives stone 9. Find the true stone.
    • Expected Result: Stone 8 (6 + 2 = 8, not 9). | Keystrokes: 6 ENTER 2 +

    Unit 02: Part-Part-Whole & Number Bonds on the Place Value Tray

    Grades: Kindergarten – Grade 1 | Instrument: StackCalc SC-6 | Visual / Block Model: Place Value Studio (Number Bond Stage & Digit Blocks 0–9) Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.K.OA.A.3, CCSS.MATH.1.OA.B.3, CCSS.MATH.1.OA.C.6

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): Place [5] in Row 1 (Part 1) and [4] in Row 0 (Part 2) of your Place Value Tray. Combine the two parts to find the Whole.
    • Verified Answer: 9 ([9] Digit Block; 5 + 4 = 9).
    • Keystrokes: 5 ENTER 4 +
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): A number bond has a Whole of 8 and a known Part of 5. Find the missing Part 2.
    • Verified Answer: Missing Part is 3 (8 - 5 = 3).
    • Keystrokes: 8 ENTER 5 -

    2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

    # Investigation Prompt Expected Solution Calculator Verification Keys
    1 To make a Whole of 6, Part 1 is 2. What is the missing Part 2? 4 6 ENTER 2 -
    2 In a number bond, Part 1 is 7 and Part 2 is 2. What is the Whole when you combine both parts? 9 7 ENTER 2 +
    3 Three parts join together: first combine 2 and 3, then add 4 to that sum. What is the Whole? 9 2 ENTER 3 + 4 +
    4 Ten birds sit on a fence. Seven fly to a tree. How many stay on the fence? 3 1 0 ENTER 7 -

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Space Station Cargo Balancing Tray

    Scenario: Astronauts balance payloads on the supply shuttle. Each cargo tray holds exactly 10 kg across two compartments (Part 1 and Part 2).

    • Case Question 1: Compartment A holds 6 kg. How many kg fit into Compartment B to make 10 kg?
    • Expected Result: 4 kg | Keystrokes: 10 ENTER 6 -
    • Case Question 2: Compartment A holds 3 kg. What is the missing cargo weight for a 10 kg tray?
    • Expected Result: 7 kg | Keystrokes: 10 ENTER 3 -
    • Case Question 3: Two small containers weigh 4 kg and 5 kg. Can they both fit in a 10 kg tray?
    • Expected Result: Yes, 9 kg total (1 kg space left). | Keystrokes: 4 ENTER 5 +
    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: Sam says 10 − 8 = 3. Use the number bond to find the correct missing part.
    • Expected Result: 2 kg (8 + 2 = 10, not 3). | Keystrokes: 10 ENTER 8 -

    Unit 03: Complements to 10 & Make-10 Regrouping with Carry Tokens

    Grades: Grade 1 | Instrument: StackCalc SC-6 | Visual / Block Model: Place Value Studio (Ten-Frame Trays & Gold [+1] Carry Tokens) Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.1.OA.C.6, CCSS.MATH.1.NBT.B.2, CCSS.MATH.2.OA.B.2

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): The Ones socket holds [6]. How many more ones are needed to complete a full 10 and trade for 1 Ten?
    • Verified Answer: 4 ones (10 - 6 = 4).
    • Keystrokes: 1 0 ENTER 6 -
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Use the Make-Ten strategy on your Place Value Tray to add 9 + 6. What is the sum?
    • Verified Answer: 15 (9 + 1 = 10, then 10 + 5 = 15).
    • Keystrokes: 9 ENTER 1 + 5 +

    2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

    # Investigation Prompt Expected Solution Calculator Verification Keys

    3 The Ones socket holds [9]. How many more ones are needed to complete 10 and regroup into 1 Ten? 1 1 0 ENTER 9 -
    4 Solve 8 + 6 by bridging through 10 on your Place Value Tray. 14 8 ENTER 2 + 4 +

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Farm Fresh Egg Carton Packaging

    Scenario: A poultry farm packages organic eggs into cartons of 10. When a carton fills, workers close the lid and pack a new carton.

    • Case Question 1: A basket has 8 brown eggs and 5 white eggs. How many full 10-cartons and loose eggs?
    • Expected Result: 1 full carton of 10 and 3 loose eggs (13 eggs). | Keystrokes: 8 ENTER 5 +
    • Case Question 2: A carton has 7 eggs. How many more eggs are needed to fill the 10-frame carton?
    • Expected Result: 3 eggs | Keystrokes: 10 ENTER 7 -
    • Case Question 3: Two nests have 9 eggs and 6 eggs. What is the total egg harvest?
    • Expected Result: 15 eggs (1 ten and 5 ones). | Keystrokes: 9 ENTER 6 +
    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: A student writes 8 + 6 = 15. Count the dots to find the true sum.
    • Expected Result: 14 eggs (8 + 2 = 10, 10 + 4 = 14). | Keystrokes: 8 ENTER 6 +

    Unit 04: Doubles, Near-Doubles & Addition Facts within 20

    Grades: Grade 1 – 2 | Instrument: StackCalc SC-6 | Visual / Block Model: Place Value Studio (Twin-Row Doubling Mat & Digit Blocks) Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.1.OA.C.6, CCSS.MATH.2.OA.B.2

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): Place [7] in both rows of your Place Value Tray to build a twin-row double. What is the sum of two 7s?
    • Verified Answer: 14 ([1] Ten and [4] Ones on the tray; 7 + 7 = 14).
    • Keystrokes: 7 ENTER +
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Use the known double 6 + 6 to solve the near-double 6 + 7.
    • Verified Answer: 13 (6 + 6 = 12, then 12 + 1 = 13).
    • Keystrokes: 6 ENTER + 1 +

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: The School Bus Morning Seating Chart

    Scenario: A school bus has two identical columns of seats. Children sit in pairs: one on the window side, one on the aisle side.

    • Case Question 1: Row 1 through 7 each have 2 students (7 on left, 7 on right). How many students total?
    • Expected Result: 14 students (7 + 7 = 14). | Keystrokes: 7 ENTER +
    • Case Question 2: 8 students sit on the left and 9 on the right. Use near-doubles to find total passengers.
    • Expected Result: 17 students ((8 + 8) + 1 = 17). | Keystrokes: 8 ENTER + 1 +
    • Case Question 3: 6 boys and 6 girls sit in the front section. How many students in the front section?
    • Expected Result: 12 students | Keystrokes: 6 ENTER +
    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: Mia doubles 9 and gets 17. Use the stack to find double 9.
    • Expected Result: 18 (9 [ENTER] [+] = 18). | Keystrokes: 9 ENTER +

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Classroom Library Book Cart

    Scenario: Student librarians organize books onto a rolling cart. Each large bin holds exactly 10 books (1 Ten), and the top rack holds single display books (Ones).

    • Case Question 1: Cart A has 4 full bins of 10 books and 7 display books. How many books are on Cart A?
    • Expected Result: 47 books | Keystrokes: 40 ENTER 7 +
    • Case Question 2: 2 full bins of 10 books are added to Cart A. How many books are on the cart now?
    • Expected Result: 67 books | Keystrokes: 47 ENTER 20 +
    • Case Question 3: Cart B starts with 86 books. Classrooms check out 3 full bins of 10 books. How many books remain?
    • Expected Result: 56 books | Keystrokes: 86 ENTER 30 -
    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: Mia counts 5 bins of 10 and 3 single books and writes 35. Find the true total!
    • Expected Result: 53 books (50 + 3 = 53, not 35). | Keystrokes: 50 ENTER 3 +

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Frog Jump Number Line Olympics

    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: Leo subtracts 16 − 9 and gets 8. Use the number line to find the true distance.
    • Expected Result: 7 meters (9 + 7 = 16, not 8). | Keystrokes: 16 ENTER 9 -

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Weather Station Thermometer Log

    Scenario: Junior meteorologists record daily high and low temperatures (in °F) and monthly rainfall totals at the school rooftop weather station.

    • Case Question 1: Monday's high was 74°F and the overnight low was 48°F. What was the temperature swing?
    • Expected Result: 26°F | Keystrokes: 74 ENTER 48 -
    • Case Question 2: Morning temperature was 56°F and rose 27°F by afternoon. What was the afternoon high?
    • Expected Result: 83°F | Keystrokes: 56 ENTER 27 +
    • Case Question 3: Rainfall was 38 mm in April and 45 mm in May. How much short of 100 mm is the 2-month total?
    • Expected Result: 17 mm | Keystrokes: 100 ENTER 38 ENTER 45 + -
    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: A student computes 82°F − 47°F = 45°F by doing 7 − 2 = 5. Correct the reading!
    • Expected Result: 35°F (unbundle 1 ten: 12 − 7 = 5, 70 − 40 = 30 -> 35°F). | Keystrokes: 82 ENTER 47 -

    Unit 03: Unit Fractions, Denominator Strips & Comparator Heights

    Grades: Grade 3 – 4 | Instrument: StackCalc SC-6 | Visual / Block Model: Fraction Studio (84 mm Proportional Towers) Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.3.NF.A.1, CCSS.MATH.3.NF.A.2

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): Place two 1/4 blocks into the comparator. What fraction height do they match?
    • Verified Answer: 2/4 = 1/2 (42.0 mm).
    • Keystrokes: . 1 . 4 ENTER . 1 . 4 +
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Compare 1/4 and 1/8. How many 1/8 blocks equal 1/4?
    • Verified Answer: 2 eighths equal 1/4 (21.0 mm).
    • Keystrokes: . 1 . 8 ENTER . 1 . 8 +

    3 Find the height of three 1/6 blocks: 1/6 + 1/6 + 1/6 = 1/2 . 1 . 6 ENTER . 1 . 6 + . 1 . 6 +
    4 Subtract 1/8 from 1/4: 1/4 − 1/8 = 1/8 . 1 . 4 ENTER . 1 . 8 -

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: Architectural Glass Window Panes

    Scenario: An architect proportions structural glass windows using unit fraction ratios.

    • Case Question 1: Clerestory vs. Transom Pane: An upper clerestory pane is 1/3 of the frame height and a transom pane is 1/4. Compare your 84 mm towers to find the fraction difference:
    • Expected Result: 7 mm = 1/12 | Keystrokes: . 1 . 3 ENTER . 1 . 4 -
    • Case Question 2: Modular Louver Stack: Each glass louver vent is 1/6 of a meter tall. How many 1/6 louver blocks stack up in the comparator well to fill a 1/2-meter window opening?
    • Expected Result: 3 sixths (14×3 = 42 mm = 1/2). | Keystrokes: . 1 . 2 ENTER . 1 . 6 ÷
    • Case Question 3: Compound Mullion Frame: The architect stacks a 1/4-meter tinted pane above a 1/8-meter acoustic sill. What total fraction of a meter is the combined assembly?
    • Expected Result: 3/8 (31.5 mm). | Keystrokes: . 1 . 4 ENTER . 1 . 8 +
    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: A junior drafter claims a 1/8-meter glass strip is taller than a 1/4-meter strip because 8 > 4. Use the 84 mm Fraction Comparator to explain why this is false:
    • Expected Result: Larger denominator means smaller pieces: 1/4 (21 mm) > 1/8 (10.5 mm). | Keystrokes: . 1 . 4 ENTER . 1 . 8 -

    Unit 06: Fraction Equivalence & Common Denominators

    Grades: Grade 4 | Instrument: StackCalc SC-6 | Visual / Block Model: Fraction Studio (Comparator Well & Proportional Towers) Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.4.NF.A.1, CCSS.MATH.4.NF.A.2

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): Test 3/6 and 4/8 in the comparator well. What single simplified fraction do both equal?
    • Verified Answer: 1/2 (42.0 mm).
    • Keystrokes: . 3 . 6
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Find the common denominator and sum of 1/2 + 1/3 on paper, then check on the stack.
    • Verified Answer: 5/6 (3/6 + 2/6 = 5/6).
    • Keystrokes: . 1 . 2 ENTER . 1 . 3 +
    • Hands-On Block Focus (Fraction Multiplication & Division):
    • Multiplication: Scaling one tower by another (e.g., 2/3 of 3/8 = 1/4 matching the 21 mm block).
    • Division (Split & Rebuild 1 Full Tower): To divide 1/2 ÷ 3/4, split the 1/2 block (42 mm) into 3 equal 1/6 blocks (÷ 3 -> 14 mm is 1/4 of the mystery tower), then stack 4 of those 1/6 blocks (× 4 -> 56 mm = 2/3) to prove why ÷(3/4) = ×(4/3)!

    2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

    # Investigation Prompt Expected Solution Calculator Verification Keys

    3 Add unlike fractions: 1/4 + 3/8 = 5/8 . 1 . 4 ENTER . 3 . 8 +
    4 Find common denominator and sum: 2/5 + 1/10 = 1/2 . 2 . 5 ENTER . 1 . 1 0 +

    Unit 07: Prime Factorization, GCF/LCM & Factor Trees

    Grades: Grade 4 – 5 | Instrument: StackCalc SC-6 | Visual / Block Model: Prime Factor Studio (Logarithmic Prime Blocks 2, 3, 5, 7) Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.4.OA.B.4, CCSS.MATH.6.NS.B.4

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: Gear Train Planetary Mechanism

    • Expected Result: 2 × 2 × 3 = 12 | Keystrokes: 2 ENTER 2 × 3 ×
    • Case Question 2: Shared Gear Pitch (GCF): An 18-tooth drive gear meshes with a 24-tooth idler gear. Find the largest shared tooth grouping GCF(18, 24):
    • Expected Result: 6 (2 × 3 = 6). | Keystrokes: 18 ENTER 24 GCF
    • Case Question 3: Full Mesh Alignment Cycle (LCM): A 12-tooth gear drives an 18-tooth gear. After how many tooth-meshes do the exact same two marked teeth meet again?
    • Expected Result: 36 | Keystrokes: 12 ENTER 18 LCM
    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: An apprentice claims a 9-tooth cog has a prime number of teeth so it cannot share factors. Divide 9 ÷ 3 to prove 9 is composite:
    • Expected Result: 3 (9 = 3 × 3, composite). | Keystrokes: 9 ENTER 3 ÷

    Unit 08: The 4-Register Stack: Four Operations & Automatic Lift

    Grades: Grade 4 – 5 | Instrument: StackCalc SC-6 | Visual / Block Model: Expression Tree Diagram & 4-Register Stack Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.5.OA.A.1, CCSS.MATH.5.OA.A.2

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): A baker bakes 15 cookies, sells 6 of them, and packs the remaining cookies equally into 3 bags. How many cookies go in each bag?
    • Verified Answer: 3 cookies per bag ((15 - 6) ÷ 3 = 3).
    • Keystrokes: 1 5 ENTER 6 - 3 ÷
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Evaluate (24 ÷ 6) + 15 by solving the division group first and then adding 15 to that result.
    • Verified Answer: 19 (24 ÷ 6 = 4, then 4 + 15 = 19).
    • Keystrokes: 2 4 ENTER 6 ÷ 1 5 +

    4 A farmer collects 20 eggs, sets aside 2 cracked eggs, and divides the rest equally among 6 cartons. How many per carton? 3 eggs 2 0 ENTER 2 - 6 ÷

    Unit 09: Adding & Subtracting Fractions & Mixed Numbers

    Grades: Grade 5 | Instrument: StackCalc SC-6 | Visual / Block Model: Fraction Studio (84 mm Proportional Towers) Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.5.NF.A.1, CCSS.MATH.5.NF.A.2

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: The Chef's Artisan Bread Recipe

    Scenario: A master baker combines sourdough flours across multiple dough troughs.

    • Case Question 1: Multi-Grain Trough Blend: The baker blends 2 1/3 cups of dark rye flour with 1 1/2 cups of whole wheat flour. Find the combined flour volume:
    • Expected Result: 3 5/6 cups | Keystrokes: 2 . 1 . 3 ENTER 1 . 1 . 2 +
    • Case Question 2: Levain Starter Portioning: A proofing tub holds 3 1/4 cups of sourdough starter. After scooping out 1 1/2 cups for baguettes, how much starter remains?
    • Expected Result: 1 3/4 cups | Keystrokes: 3 . 1 . 4 ENTER 1 . 1 . 2 -
    • Case Question 3: Brioche Hydration Mix: Combine 1 3/8 cups of warm buttermilk and 2 1/4 cups of whole milk in the mixer bowl. What is the total liquid volume?
    • Expected Result: 3 5/8 cups | Keystrokes: 1 . 3 . 8 ENTER 2 . 1 . 4 +
    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: Subtracting 1 1/2 cups of flour from 3 1/4 cups, an apprentice subtracted 1/2 − 1/4 backward and wrote 2 1/4 cups. Trade 1 Whole for 4/4 to fix it:
    • Expected Result: 1 3/4 (must unbundle 1 whole to make 2 5/4 − 1 2/4 = 1 3/4). | Keystrokes: 3 . 1 . 4 ENTER 1 . 1 . 2 -

    Unit 10: Fraction Studio: Proportions, Multiplication & Tower Division

    Grades: Grade 5 | Instrument: StackCalc SC-6 | Visual / Block Model: Fraction Studio (84 mm Proportional Towers) Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.5.NF.B.4, CCSS.MATH.5.NF.B.7

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): Find 2/3 of 3/4 by multiplying (2/3) × (3/4) and simplifying to lowest terms.
    • Verified Answer: 1/2 (6/12 = 1/2, 42.0 mm).
    • Keystrokes: .2.3 ENTER .3.4 ×
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Use Split & Rebuild to evaluate (3/4) ÷ (3/8) on paper and check on your SC-6.
    • Verified Answer: 2 ((3/4) × (8/3) = 2 wholes).
    • Keystrokes: .3.4 ENTER .3.8 ÷
    • Hands-On Block Focus (Fraction Multiplication & Division):
    • Multiplication: Scaling one tower by another (e.g., 2/3 of 3/8 = 1/4 matching the 21 mm block).
    • Division (Split & Rebuild 1 Full Tower): To divide 1/2 ÷ 3/4, split the 1/2 block (42 mm) into 3 equal 1/6 blocks (÷ 3 -> 14 mm is 1/4 of the mystery tower), then stack 4 of those 1/6 blocks (× 4 -> 56 mm = 2/3) to prove why ÷(3/4) = ×(4/3)!

    2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

    # Investigation Prompt Expected Solution Calculator Verification Keys
    1 Multiply fraction scaling: 1/2 × 3/4 = 3/8 .1.2 ENTER .3.4 ×
    2 Multiply fractions and simplify: 2/3 × 3/8 = 1/4 .2.3 ENTER .3.8 ×
    3 Divide by a whole number: 1/2 ÷ 3 = 1/6 .1.2 ENTER 3 ÷
    4 Divide by a fraction (Split & Rebuild): 1/2 ÷ 3/4 = 2/3 .1.2 ENTER .3.4 ÷

    5. STEM Lab Case Study: Suspension Bridge Tower Cable Geometry

    Scenario: Civil engineers calculate tower tension ratios using fraction multiplication and division.

    • Case Question 1: Secondary Suspender Scaling: A main bridge cable carries 3/4 of a kilonewton unit load, and a secondary suspender carries 1/2 of that load (1/2 × 3/4). Find the suspender load:
    • Expected Result: 3/8 meter (31.5 mm). | Keystrokes: . 1 . 2 ENTER . 3 . 4 ×
    • Case Question 2: Three-Strand Load Sharing: A 1/2-meter anchor rod is split equally among 3 steel tie-down brackets (1/2 ÷ 3). What fraction of a meter does each bracket hold?
    • Expected Result: 1/6 (14.0 mm). | Keystrokes: . 1 . 2 ENTER 3 ÷
    • Case Question 3: Full Tower Reconstruction: A 1/2-meter scale test cable represents 3/4 of the design span. Use Split & Rebuild (1/2 ÷ 3/4) to find the full 1-tower design span:
    • Expected Result: 2/3 (56.0 mm tower). | Keystrokes: . 1 . 2 ENTER . 3 . 4 ÷
    • Case Question 4: Error Analysis: A structural intern computed 1/2 ÷ 3/4 = 3/8 by multiplying instead of dividing. Use the 84 mm Split & Rebuild model to explain why 1/2 ÷ 3/4 = 2/3:
    • Expected Result: Dividing by 3/4 scales up by 4/3: 1/2 × 4/3 = 2/3 (56 mm). | Keystrokes: . 1 . 2 ENTER . 3 . 4 ÷

    Unit 01: Linear Equations, Rate of Change & Slope Pipelines

    Grades: Grade 9 – 10 (Algebra 1) | Instrument: StackCalc SC-32 | Visual / Block Model: Coordinate Slope Triangle Diagram & 4-Level Stack Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.HSA.CED.A.2, CCSS.MATH.HSF.IF.B.6

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): Find the slope passing through (1, 4) and (5, 16) on paper, then check on SC-32.
    • Verified Answer: 3 (12 ÷ 4 = 3).
    • Keystrokes: 16 ENTER 4 - 5 ENTER 1 - ÷
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Given line with m = 3 through (2, 4), find y when x = 8.
    • Verified Answer: 22 (3(8−2) + 4 = 18 + 4 = 22).
    • Keystrokes: 8 ENTER 2 - 3 × 4 +

    Unit 02: Algebraic Factoring, Area Models & Quadratic Trinomials

    Grades: Grade 9 – 10 (Algebra 1) | Instrument: StackCalc SC-32 | Visual / Block Model: Prime Factor Studio (Factor Pairs) & 2×2 Algebraic Area Grid Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.HSA.SSE.B.3, CCSS.MATH.HSA.REI.B.4

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): Factor x² + 7x + 12 by decomposing 12 with Prime Factor blocks to find the factor pair whose sum is 7, and find both roots of x² + 7x + 12 = 0.
    • Verified Answer: 3 × 4 = 12 and 3 + 4 = 7; factored form (x + 3)(x + 4), roots x = -3 and x = -4.
    • Keystrokes: POLY 1 B 7 C 1 2 D XEQ
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Factor x² - 5x + 6 into two binomials by finding two negative factors of +6 whose sum is -5, and state both roots.
    • Verified Answer: (-2) × (-3) = +6 and (-2) + (-3) = -5; factored form (x - 2)(x - 3), roots x = 2 and x = 3.
    • Keystrokes: POLY 1 B 5 +/- C 6 D XEQ

    2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

    # Investigation Prompt Expected Solution Calculator Verification Keys
    1 Factor x² + 6x + 8 by decomposing 8 into a factor pair that sums to 6, and find the roots. (x + 2)(x + 4); roots -2, -4 POLY 1 B 6 C 8 D XEQ
    2 Factor x² - 4 (difference of two squares, where the linear coefficient b = 0). (x - 2)(x + 2); roots 2, -2 POLY 1 B 0 C 4 +/- D XEQ
    3 Factor x² + 5x + 4 into two binomials and state the solutions to x² + 5x + 4 = 0. (x + 1)(x + 4); roots -1, -4 POLY 1 B 5 C 4 D XEQ
    4 Factor x² - 5x + 6 using signed factor pairs and state both roots. (x - 2)(x - 3); roots 2, 3 POLY 1 B 5 +/- C 6 D XEQ

    Unit 03: The Quadratic Formula & Automatic [POLY] Root Extraction

    Unit 04: Polynomial Operations, Horner's Rule & Synthetic Division

    Grades: Grade 10 – 11 (Algebra 2) | Instrument: StackCalc SC-32 | Visual / Block Model: Nested Polynomial Pipeline Diagram & SC-32 Stack Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.HSA.APR.A.1, CCSS.MATH.HSA.APR.B.2

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): Evaluate P(x) = 3x³ − 2x² + 4x − 8 at x = 2 using Horner's rule on the stack.
    • Verified Answer: 16 (((3(2)−2)(2)+4)(2)−8 = (4(2)+4)(2)−8 = 12(2)−8 = 16).
    • Keystrokes: 3 ENTER 2 × -2 + 2 × 4 + 2 × -8 +
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Test if (x − 3) is a factor of P(x) = x³ − 4x² + x + 6 using synthetic division on stack.
    • Verified Answer: Remainder = 0; (x − 3) is a factor.
    • Keystrokes: 1 ENTER 3 × -4 + 3 × 1 + 3 × 6 +

    Unit 05: Exponential Growth, Decay & Continuous Compounding

    Grades: Grade 10 – 12 (Algebra 2 & Precalculus) | Instrument: StackCalc SC-32 | Visual / Block Model: Prime Factor Studio & SC-32 Scientific Stack ([yˣ], [eˣ]) Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.HSF.LE.A.1, CCSS.MATH.HSF.LE.A.4

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): A bacteria culture starts with P = 500 cells and triples every hour. Calculate the population after t = 4 hours.
    • Verified Answer: 3^4 = 81; 500 × 81 = 40,500 bacteria.
    • Keystrokes: 3 ENTER 4 yˣ 5 0 0 ×
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): A bacterial colony of 500 cells grows continuously at rate r = 0.12 per hour. Find population at t = 8 hrs.
    • Verified Answer: 1,305.85 cells (500 × e^0.96 ≈ 1,305.85).
    • Keystrokes: 0.12 ENTER 8 × eˣ 500 ×

    Unit 06: Natural Logarithms, Inverse Exponentials & Doubling Time [LN]

    Grades: Grade 11 – 12 (Algebra 2 & Precalc) | Instrument: StackCalc SC-32 | Visual / Block Model: Prime Factor Studio (Logarithmic Height Towers) & SC-32 [LN] Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.HSF.BF.B.5, CCSS.MATH.HSF.LE.A.4

    Unit 08: 2D Vectors, Polar Conversions & Resultant Forces

    Grades: Grade 11 – 12 (Precalc & Physics) | Instrument: StackCalc SC-32 | Visual / Block Model: 2D Vector Right-Triangle Diagram & SC-32 [▸θ,r] Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.HSN.VM.A.1, CCSS.MATH.HSN.VM.A.3

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): A surveyor walks 60 m East (x = 60) and 80 m North (y = 80). Find straight-line distance r and angle θ.

    • Keystrokes: 80 ENTER 60 ▸θ,r
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Add vectors A = <6, 8> and B = <2, 7>. Find resultant magnitude and angle.
    • Verified Answer: r = 17.0, θ = 61.93° (<8, 15> -> 8²+15²=289, √289=17).
    • Keystrokes: 8 ENTER 7 + 6 ENTER 2 + ▸θ,r

    Unit 09: Combinatorics, Probability Ratios & Series Accumulation

    Grades: Grade 11 – 12 & AP (Precalc & AP Stats) | Instrument: StackCalc SC-32 | Visual / Block Model: Decision Slot Diagram, Statistical Plot & SC-32 [PROB] ([Pn,r]/[Cn,r])/[Σ+] Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.HSS.CP.B.9

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): A lottery draws 4 winning numbers from a pool of 10 (order does not matter). How many winning combinations are possible?
    • Verified Answer: (10 × 9 × 8 × 7) / (4 × 3 × 2 × 1) = 5040 / 24 = 210 combinations.
    • Keystrokes: 1 0 x! 4 x! ÷ 6 x! ÷
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Given the four data points {12, 15, 18, 21}, find the sample size n, the total sum of the values, and the sample mean x̄.
    • Verified Answer: Count n = 4, Sum = 66, Mean x̄ = 66 / 4 = 16.5.
    • Keystrokes: 12 Σ+ 15 Σ+ 18 Σ+ 21 Σ+ x̄,ȳ

    2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

    # Investigation Prompt Expected Solution Calculator Verification Keys
    1 Evaluate 6! (the number of ways to arrange 6 distinct books on a shelf). 6 × 5 × 4 × 3 × 2 × 1 = 720 6 x!
    2 How many ways can 5 runners finish in 1st, 2nd, and 3rd place? 5 × 4 × 3 = 60 5 x! 2 x! ÷
    3 How many 2-person partnerships can be formed from 7 students? (7 × 6) / 2 = 21 7 x! 2 x! ÷ 5 x! ÷
    4 Find the mean x̄ of the two data values 5 and 15. (5 + 15) / 2 = 10 5 Σ+ 1 5 Σ+ x̄,ȳ

    Unit 10: Digital Logic, Base Conversions & Computer Architecture

    Grades: Grade 9 – 12 & AP (AP CSP & CS) | Instrument: StackCalc SC-32 | Visual / Block Model: Place Value Studio (Binary [0]/[1] Bit Sockets) & SC-32 [BASE] Key Standards: CCSS.MATH.HSN.RN.A.1, CSTA 3A-DA-07

    1. Concept Labs & Warm-Up Solutions

    • Warm-Up A (Core Lab): Express decimal 255 as a sum of powers of 2 to write its 8-bit binary and 2-digit hexadecimal representations.
    • Verified Answer: Binary: 11111111_2 (eight 1s); Hexadecimal: FF_16 (15 × 16 + 15 = 255).
    • Keystrokes: 2 5 5 BASE
    • Warm-Up B (Extension Lab): Compare 12 and 3 column by column in 4-bit binary to find the result of 12 OR 3 in both binary and decimal.
    • Verified Answer: 1100_2 OR 0011_2 = 1111_2 = 15_10.
    • Keystrokes: 1 2 ENTER 3 +

    2. Hands-On Lab Investigations (Investigations 1–4)

    # Investigation Prompt Expected Solution Calculator Verification Keys
    1 Decompose decimal 42 into powers of 2 to write its 6-bit binary representation. 32 + 8 + 2 -> 101010_2 4 2 BASE
    2 Convert hexadecimal 2A_16 to decimal using place value. 32 + 10 = 42 2 ENTER 1 6 × 1 0 + BASE
    3 Evaluate the bitwise AND of 15 and 7 in binary and decimal. 0111_2 = 7 1 5 ENTER 8 ÷R x⇄y
    4 Evaluate the bitwise XOR of 12 and 10 in binary and decimal. 0110_2 = 6 4 ENTER 2 +

    W1 Sum of roots of x² - 9x + 20 = 0 : Vieta's rule: -b / a = 9 / 1 9
    W2 Product of roots of x² + 11x + 24 = 0 : Vieta's rule: c / a = 24 / 1 24
    W3 Vertex x-coord of y = x² - 8x + 19 : Axis of symmetry x = -b / (2a) = 8 / 2 4
    W4 Solve for x: x + y = 12, x - y = 6 : Average (12 + 6) ÷ 2 9
    W5 Hypotenuse for legs 7 and 24 = Pythagorean triple (7, 24, 25) 25
    W6 3D diagonal √(2² + 3² + 6²) = √(4 + 9 + 36) = √49 7

    3. Optimal Champion Pipelines

    • 8C #5 (Roots of x²+5x+6=0): [↱][POLY] 1 [B] 5 [C] 6 [D] [XEQ] -> -3, -2
    • 8D #5 (Sum of roots 2x²-10x+7): Vieta's -b/a = 10 / 2 = 5
    • 8D #10 (Min of x²-8x+19): Complete square: (x - 4)² + 3 -> min is 3
    • 8D #18 (√(3²+4²+12²)): Chained (3,4,5) + (5,12,13) -> 13

    1. Warm-Up Drills (W1–W6)

    W1 3/4 + (1/2 × 1/4) (decimal) = 0.75 + 0.125 0.875
    W2 (2/3 + 3/4) × 12 = Distribute 12: 8 + 9 17
    W3 Distance from (3, 4) to (9, 12) = dx = 6, dy = 8 -> (6, 8, 10) triple 10
    W4 Sum of roots of 2x² - 14x + 9 = 0 : Vieta's -b / a = 14 / 2 7
    W5 Minimum value of y = x² - 6x + 14 : (x - 3)² + 5 -> min is 5 5
    W6 2/3 × 3/4 × 4/5 × 5/6 = Telescoping cancel -> 2/6 = 1/3 1/3

    Section 9: Section 9: Trigonometry, Vectors & Polar Coordinates

    Grades: Grades 8–12+ | Focus: Special angles, right-triangle projections, inverse trig, and vector magnitudes.

    1. Warm-Up Drills (W1–W6)

    Drill Prompt Shortcut Cue Verified Answer
    W1 Vector <5, 12> -> Magnitude r = Pythagorean triple or [12][ENTER][5][▸θ,r] 13
    W2 5 sin²(20°) + 5 cos²(20°) = Factor 5(sin² + cos²) = 5(1) 5
    W3 Area of Δ: a = 8, b = 10, C = 30° -> 1/2 × a × b × sin(30°) = 0.5 × 80 × 0.5 20
    W4 2 sin(15°) cos(15°) = Double-angle identity: sin(30°) 0.5
    W5 max value of 3 sin(x) + 4 cos(x) = Hypotenuse √(3² + 4²) 5
    W6 Law of Cosines: a=8, b=5, C=60° -> c = c² = 64 + 25 - 2(8)(5)(0.5) = 49 -> c = 7 7

    1. Warm-Up Drills (W1–W6)

    Drill Prompt Shortcut Cue Verified Answer
    W1 log₁₀(4) + log₁₀(25) = log₁₀(4 × 25) = log₁₀(100) 2
    W2 Half-life (3 periods): 100 × (0.5)³ = 100 ÷ 8 = 12.5 12.5
    W3 27^(2/3) = Cube root first (3), then square (3²) 9
    W4 16^(3/4) = 4th root first (2), then cube (2³) 8
    W5 Change of base: ln(125) ÷ ln(5) = log₅(125) = 3 since 5³ = 125 3
    W6 Solve: 4^x = 32 (decimal) = Base 2: 2^(2x) = 2⁵ -> 2x = 5 2.5

    W2 8C3 = (8 × 7 × 6) ÷ 6 = 8 × 7 56
    W3 Arrangements of BOOK (4! ÷ 2!) = 24 ÷ 2 12
    W4 Row 5 sum: 5C0 + ... + 5C5 = Pascal's Row Sum = 2⁵ 32
    W5 5 coins: P(exactly 2 Heads) = 5C2 ÷ 2⁵ = 10 ÷ 32 0.3125
    W6 Sample SD of {2, 5, 8} = 3 evenly spaced numbers -> step d = 3 3

    W1 Hex 2F₁₆ in decimal = 2 × 16 + 15 = 32 + 15 47
    W2 12 (1100₂) XOR 10 (1010₂) = Differing bits: 0110₂ 6
    W3 Left shift: 7 << 3 = 7 × 2³ = 7 × 8 56
    W4 Binary 10101100₂ in hexadecimal = Nibbles 1010 (A) and 1100 (C) AC
    W5 Decimal 4095 (2¹² - 1) in Hex = Three 4-bit 1111 nibbles -> FFF FFF
    W6 8-bit Two's Complement of -1 (Hex) = 256 - 1 = 255 = FF₁₆ FF
